# 9. NUMERICAL CORE — FULL IMPLEMENTATION

Deepen the numerical foundation.

Implement or harden applicable existing functionality for:

## Floating-point utilities

- scale-aware tolerances;
- stable norms;
- finite checks;
- relative/absolute comparisons;
- compensated summation;
- overflow-safe operations;
- stable polynomial evaluation;
- safe normalization;
- machine epsilon handling;
- scaling/unscaling.

## Linear algebra

Deep implementations where architecture supports them:

- LU;
- QR;
- Cholesky;
- SVD;
- eigensolvers;
- least squares;
- pseudo-inverse;
- condition estimates;
- iterative refinement;
- matrix norms;
- residual calculations.

Avoid:

```
inv(A) @ b
```

where:

```
solve(A, b)
```

is mathematically preferable.

---

# 10. SPARSE NUMERICAL INFRASTRUCTURE

Move sparse capabilities beyond conceptual API level.

Implement meaningful support for:

- COO;
- CSR;
- CSC;
- sparse matrix construction;
- sparse matvec;
- sparse matrix operations;
- sparse triangular operations;
- preconditioning interfaces;
- iterative sparse solves;
- residual monitoring;
- convergence histories.

Applicable Krylov methods may include:

- CG;
- GMRES;
- BiCGSTAB;
- MINRES;
- LSQR;

only when mathematically appropriate.

Do not register an algorithm unless its implementation actually exists.

---

# 11. PRECONDITIONING

Deepen applicable preconditioners:

- Jacobi;
- block Jacobi;
- SSOR;
- ILU-like approaches;
- incomplete Cholesky where valid;
- domain decomposition abstractions;
- algebraic interfaces.

Measure:

[
r\_k=b-Ax\_k
]

and report convergence using actual residuals.

Never report success merely because iteration count ended.

---

# 12. ROOT FINDING

Fully implement applicable root-solving infrastructure.

Include robust versions where supported:

- bisection;
- secant;
- Newton;
- safeguarded Newton;
- Brent-like hybrid methods.

Test:

- exact root;
- no bracket;
- zero derivative;
- flat function;
- discontinuity;
- maximum iteration;
- extremely small root;
- extremely large root;
- badly scaled function;
- NaN-producing function.

---

# 13. NONLINEAR SYSTEM SOLVERS

Move beyond scalar root solving.

Develop:

[
F(x)=0
]

support with:

- Newton systems;
- finite-difference Jacobians;
- analytic Jacobians;
- line search;
- damping;
- trust mechanisms where architecture supports them;
- convergence monitoring;
- singular-Jacobian detection.

---

# 14. OPTIMIZATION ENGINE

Convert optimization abstractions into real optimizers.

Cover applicable existing methods such as:

- gradient descent;
- momentum;
- conjugate-gradient optimization;
- BFGS;
- L-BFGS;
- Newton;
- trust region;
- projected methods;
- constrained methods;
- derivative-free optimization.

Every optimizer needs:

```
objective evaluations
gradient evaluations
iteration count
termination reason
final gradient norm
constraint violation
history
```

Do not return only the final vector.

---

# 15. CONSTRAINED OPTIMIZATION

Where already architecturally represented, deepen support for:

[
\min\_x f(x)
]

subject to:

[
g\_i(x)\le0
]

[
h\_j(x)=0
]

Implement constraint evaluation and violation metrics.

Measure:

# [ v(x)

\sum\_i \max(0,g\_i(x))
\+
\sum\_j |h\_j(x)|
]

where appropriate.

---

# 16. AUTOMATIC DIFFERENTIATION / DIFFERENTIATION INFRASTRUCTURE

If SM31 contains differentiation architecture, convert it into actual operations.

Support applicable:

- forward differentiation;
- reverse differentiation;
- Jacobian-vector products;
- vector-Jacobian products;
- finite-difference validation.

Cross-check derivatives against high-quality finite differences.

---

# 17. ODE INFRASTRUCTURE

Deepen ODE integration.

Implement applicable:

- Euler;
- midpoint;
- RK4;
- adaptive Runge-Kutta;
- embedded error estimation;
- event detection;
- dense output if supported;
- stiff-handling paths if architecture includes them.

Test known analytical problems.

For example:

[
y'=\lambda y
]

with exact:

[
y(t)=y\_0e^{\lambda t}
]

Measure actual convergence order.

---

# 18. PDE INFRASTRUCTURE

Every PDE abstraction must connect to an executable discretization or be clearly classified.

Support existing architecture for:

- finite difference;
- finite volume;
- finite element;
- time stepping;
- spatial operators;
- boundary conditions;
- source terms;
- residual calculation.

---

# 19. CFD — TRANSFORM REFERENCE SOLVERS INTO DEEP SOLVERS

SM32 must significantly deepen CFD.

Preserve the repaired SM31 QP1 second-order Euler path.

Develop applicable existing infrastructure for:

## Conservation laws

# [ \frac{\partial U}{\partial t} + \nabla\cdot F(U)

S(U)
]

Ensure actual conservation accounting.

## Reconstruction

Applicable:

- first order;
- MUSCL;
- TVD;
- minmod;
- MC;
- van Leer;
- higher-order reconstruction already present.

## Fluxes

Where architecturally present:

- Rusanov;
- HLL;
- HLLC;
- Roe-type formulations if properly safeguarded.

Do not add a flux name without implementing its equations.

---

# 20. CFD MULTIDIMENSIONAL DEPTH

If multidimensional CFD APIs exist, convert them from facade/reference level toward actual 2-D/3-D operations.

Require:

- multidimensional mesh indexing;
- face normals;
- conservative face fluxes;
- boundary faces;
- volume updates;
- CFL calculation;
- geometry consistency.

---

# 21. NAVIER–STOKES DEPTH

If Navier–Stokes functionality is represented in SM31, implement the actual viscous contributions.

Include applicable:

- stress tensor;
- viscosity;
- thermal diffusion;
- energy transport.

Do not label Euler-only behavior as Navier–Stokes.

---

# 22. CFD BOUNDARY CONDITIONS

Make boundary conditions real first-class objects.

Applicable:

- transmissive;
- reflective;
- wall;
- slip wall;
- no-slip wall;
- periodic;
- inflow;
- outflow;
- prescribed state.

Each needs dedicated regression tests.

---

# 23. CFD PHYSICAL ADMISSIBILITY

Continuously enforce or validate:

[
\rho>0
]

[

]

[
e>0
]

Reject:

```
NaN
Inf
nonphysical primitive states
invalid gamma
invalid cell geometry
```

---

# 24. CFD VERIFICATION MATRIX

Run canonical cases appropriate to implemented equations.

Examples:

- uniform flow;
- Sod shock tube;
- contact discontinuity;
- smooth advection;
- acoustic perturbation;
- shock interaction cases where supported;
- manufactured solutions.

Measure:

- conservation;
- error norms;
- grid convergence;
- positivity;
- runtime;
- stability.

---

# 25. FEA — FULL IMPLEMENTATION DEPTH

Upgrade finite-element architecture beyond reference scale.

Every implemented element should define:

```
shape functions
shape derivatives
Jacobian
strain-displacement matrix
constitutive relation
element stiffness
element force
assembly
boundary conditions
solver
post-processing
```

---

# 26. FEA ELEMENT LIBRARY

Where architecture permits, deepen existing elements rather than merely naming more.

Potential families:

- truss;
- beam;
- frame;
- triangle;
- quadrilateral;
- tetrahedral;
- hexahedral.

Only advertise an element if its stiffness/force formulation and tests exist.

---

# 27. FEA ASSEMBLY

Implement true global assembly.

Given:

[
K\_e
]

construct:

[
K
]

with correct local-to-global mappings.

Verify:

[
K\approx K^T
]

where symmetry is expected.

---

# 28. FEA BOUNDARY CONDITIONS

Implement:

- fixed DOF;
- prescribed displacement;
- nodal force;
- distributed loading where supported;
- mixed constraints.

Detect underconstrained rigid-body systems.

---

# 29. FEA MATERIAL MODELS

Where architecture contains material modeling, ensure actual constitutive equations exist.

At minimum for applicable linear elasticity:

[
\sigma=C\epsilon
]

Do not label a material model implemented if it is only metadata.

---

# 30. FEA VALIDATION

Use analytical/reference cases such as:

- axial bar;
- cantilever;
- simple truss;
- patch tests;
- mesh-convergence problems.

Calculate:

[
e\_h=|u\_h-u\_{\mathrm{ref}}|
]

Perform grid refinement where possible.

---

# 31. COMPUTATIONAL GEOMETRY

Deepen all geometry primitives.

Require robust handling of:

- points;
- vectors;
- segments;
- planes;
- intersections;
- projections;
- distances;
- orientation;
- bounding volumes;
- tolerance-aware predicates.

Prevent catastrophic failures for nearly degenerate geometry.

---

# 32. B-SPLINE / CURVE INFRASTRUCTURE

Preserve the QP1 curvature repair.

Deepen:

- basis evaluation;
- derivatives;
- curvature;
- knot handling;
- endpoint handling;
- repeated knots;
- arc length;
- closest-point queries where represented.

---

# 33. CAD-KERNEL DEPTH

Where CAD architecture exists, move beyond primitive storage.

Implement applicable topology concepts:

```
vertex
edge
wire
face
shell
solid
```

Maintain explicit relationships.

Do not call an unordered triangle set a B-rep solid.

---

# 34. GEOMETRIC ROBUSTNESS

Audit tolerance handling.

Avoid:

```
if x == 0:
```

for geometric floating-point predicates where scale-aware logic is required.

Develop centralized geometric tolerance policy.

---

# 35. MESH INFRASTRUCTURE

Deepen:

- topology;
- adjacency;
- connectivity;
- normals;
- quality metrics;
- orientation;
- boundary extraction;
- element lookup;
- refinement infrastructure.

Detect:

- inverted cells;
- zero-volume cells;
- duplicate nodes;
- disconnected elements;
- invalid orientation.

---

# 36. MESH QUALITY

Implement actual quality metrics appropriate to element type.

Track values such as:

- aspect ratio;
- skewness;
- Jacobian determinant;
- angle quality;
- volume/area.

Provide deterministic failure or warnings for invalid cells.

---

# 37. MULTIPHYSICS

Turn coupling architecture into actual coupling algorithms.

For coupled subsystems:

[
F\_1(x,y)=0
]

[
F\_2(x,y)=0
]

support applicable:

- sequential coupling;
- fixed-point coupling;
- under-relaxation;
- residual monitoring;
- convergence criteria.

Record coupling histories.

---

# 38. CONSERVATION ACROSS COUPLED PHYSICS

Where quantities transfer between solvers, explicitly measure transferred conserved quantities.

Examples:

- force;
- energy;
- mass;
- heat flux.

Avoid opaque handoff APIs.

---

# 39. DIGITAL TWIN

Deepen digital-twin functionality into a stateful computational system.

A valid twin should contain:

```
state
observations
parameters
prediction
update
uncertainty
residual
history
timestamp
provenance
```

---

# 40. STATE ESTIMATION

Where architecture supports it, deepen:

- filtering;
- parameter updates;
- prediction/correction;
- covariance handling;
- innovation residuals.

Validate on synthetic systems with known ground truth.

---

# 41. UNCERTAINTY QUANTIFICATION

Implement actual UQ calculations where represented.

Applicable:

- Monte Carlo;
- Latin hypercube;
- polynomial approximations;
- bootstrap;
- sensitivity indices;
- confidence intervals.

Report actual sampling errors.

---

# 42. PROBABILISTIC NUMERICS

Where probabilistic components exist:

- validate distributions;
- normalize probabilities;
- validate covariance;
- reject impossible parameters;
- seed stochastic processes;
- expose reproducibility controls.

---

# 43. STATISTICS

Deepen numerical statistics:

- stable mean;
- variance;
- covariance;
- robust location;
- robust scale;
- correlation;
- quantiles.

Test against known values.

---

# 44. SIGNAL / SPECTRAL METHODS

If represented:

- FFT;
- spectral transforms;
- filtering;
- convolution;
- correlation;
- frequency analysis;

must be executable and benchmarked.

Test Parseval-type relationships where applicable.

---

# 45. GRAPH ALGORITHMS

Any graph-analysis registrations must correspond to functioning algorithms.

Potential existing domains:

- BFS;
- DFS;
- shortest path;
- connected components;
- centrality;
- spanning trees;
- flow.

Validate graph invariants.

---

# 46. SEARCH AND PLANNING

If SM31 includes search/planning architecture, implement actual state expansion.

Require:

```
state representation
actions
transition
cost
heuristic
frontier
visited structure
termination
path reconstruction
```

Support algorithms already represented such as:

- BFS;
- Dijkstra;
- A\*;
- tree search.

---

# 47. MACHINE LEARNING PRIMITIVES

Do not claim PyTorch/JAX-scale capability merely because matrix operations exist.

But where ML modules are present, fully implement their declared scope.

Potential primitives:

- dense layers;
- activation functions;
- losses;
- optimizers;
- forward propagation;
- gradients;
- training loops.

Test numerical gradients.

---

# 48. ATTENTION INFRASTRUCTURE

If attention mechanisms are represented, implement mathematically correct attention.

For example:

# [ A(Q,K,V)

\operatorname{softmax}
\left(
\frac{QK^T}{\sqrt{d}}
\right)V
]

Require:

- masking;
- stable softmax;
- shape validation;
- numerical tests.

Do not claim FlashAttention unless IO-aware fused execution is actually implemented and benchmarked.

---

# 49. DIFFUSION / GENERATIVE PRIMITIVES

If diffusion architecture exists, distinguish:

- equation primitives;
- noise schedules;
- reverse process;
- learned denoiser;
- training infrastructure;
- sampling.

Do not describe mathematical primitives as equivalent to a trained foundation model.

---

# 50. REINFORCEMENT LEARNING / SEARCH

If RL/search capability exists, require actual:

```
environment
state
action
reward
policy
value
trajectory
update
evaluation
```

For MCTS:

```
selection
expansion
simulation/evaluation
backup
visit counts
action selection
```

A class named `MCTS` without these operations is not implementation.

---

# 51. DISCOVERY ALGORITHMS

For AlphaTensor/AlphaDev-like algorithm-discovery primitives, maintain claim discipline.

Actual discovery requires:

- search space;
- executable candidates;
- objective;
- evaluator;
- mutation/action system;
- search;
- reproducible discovered result.

Do not equate a generic optimizer with autonomous algorithm discovery.

---

# 52. HPC BACKEND

SM32 should transform HPC abstractions into measurable execution paths.

Provide backend capabilities where environment supports them:

```
serial
threaded CPU
multiprocess
vectorized
MPI
GPU
```

Each backend gets status:

```
IMPLEMENTED
TESTED
BENCHMARKED
UNAVAILABLE_ENVIRONMENT
```

---

# 53. MPI

If MPI is available, perform real distributed operations.

Test:

- rank discovery;
- broadcast;
- scatter;
- gather;
- reduce;
- allreduce;
- halo-style communication where solver architecture supports it.

Run actual multi-process tests.

If MPI cannot run:

```
MPI = IMPLEMENTED_UNEXECUTED
```

not:

```
MPI = VERIFIED
```

---

# 54. GPU

GPU capabilities require actual device execution.

A NumPy path wrapped in a GPU API is not GPU implementation.

Measure:

```
device
kernel execution
host-to-device transfer
device-to-host transfer
runtime
memory
```

If no supported GPU is available, preserve code and report:

```
GPU_RUNTIME_VALIDATION = NOT_EXECUTED
```

---

# 55. PERFORMANCE ENGINEERING

Profile before optimizing.

Measure:

- wall time;
- CPU time;
- peak memory;
- allocation count where practical;
- throughput;
- scaling.

Identify real hotspots.

Then optimize.

---

# 56. VECTORIZATION

Replace unnecessarily scalar numerical loops with appropriate vector operations only when:

- semantics remain equivalent;
- memory use remains acceptable;
- tests prove correctness;
- performance improves measurably.

Never vectorize solely for appearance.

---

# 57. CACHE / MEMORY AWARENESS

For large numerical kernels consider:

- contiguous arrays;
- memory layout;
- block operations;
- temporary allocation reduction;
- cache-friendly loops;
- chunking.

Benchmark changes.

---

# 58. PARALLEL SCALING

For available parallel backends measure:

[
S\_p=\frac{T\_1}{T\_p}
]

and:

[
E\_p=\frac{S\_p}{p}
]

Report real values.

Do not infer scaling.

---

# 59. MEMORY COMPLEXITY

For major algorithms document empirical and theoretical memory characteristics.

Where possible:

[
M(n)=O(f(n))
]

Confirm using increasing problem sizes.

---

# 60. ALGORITHMIC COMPLEXITY

For major kernels document expected:

[
T(n)=O(f(n))
]

and compare to empirical measurements.

Flag pathological implementations.

---

# 61. INPUT VALIDATION

Every public API must reject structurally invalid inputs.

Test:

- empty arrays;
- wrong dimensions;
- ragged arrays;
- NaN;
- Inf;
- invalid enums;
- impossible geometry;
- negative physical quantities;
- zero denominators;
- invalid mesh sizes;
- incompatible dimensions.

---

# 62. FAILURE SEMANTICS

Do not collapse every problem into `ValueError`.

Maintain or create a coherent numerical error taxonomy.

Possible categories:

```
InvalidInput
DimensionMismatch
NonPhysicalState
DegenerateGeometry
SingularSystem
IllConditionedSystem
ConvergenceFailure
CFLViolation
InvariantViolation
BackendUnavailable
ResourceFailure
NumericalOverflow
```

Keep backward compatibility where possible.

---

# 63. INVARIANT ENGINE

Create a reusable invariant system.

Examples:

```
finite values
positive density
positive pressure
matrix symmetry
probability normalization
mesh orientation
energy bounds
conservation
monotonic iteration count
valid dimensions
```

Allow solvers to emit invariant telemetry.

---

# 64. OBSERVABILITY

Every major long-running solver should expose:

```
iterations
residual
convergence status
runtime
termination cause
warnings
backend
memory estimate
```

Avoid opaque numerical execution.

---

# 65. TEST ARCHITECTURE

Create test levels:

```
L0 — import/compile
L1 — unit
L2 — edge cases
L3 — property
L4 — numerical verification
L5 — integration
L6 — cross-module
L7 — stress
L8 — endurance
L9 — benchmark
L10 — reproduction
```

---

# 66. COVERAGE TARGET

SM31 QP1 preserved-source measurement was not equivalent to perfect coverage.

SM32 should target meaningful coverage rather than deleting shadowed lineage.

Target active authoritative SM32 runtime:

```
statement coverage >= 98%
branch coverage >= 95%
```

Critical solver kernels:

```
statement >= 99%
branch >= 97%
```

Coverage exclusions must be explicitly justified.

---

# 67. DO NOT GAME COVERAGE

Forbidden:

- deleting untested code merely to improve coverage;
- marking large production sections `pragma: no cover`;
- testing only imports;
- asserting only non-None values;
- calling functions without validating results.

Coverage is evidence of execution, not proof of correctness.

---

# 68. MUTATION TESTING

Introduce mutations conceptually equivalent to:

```
+ → -
* → /
< → >
<= → >=
index i → i+1
flux sign inversion
normal reversal
constraint bypass
limiter disable
CFL bypass
positivity bypass
```

Tests should catch them.

Target useful mutation score:

```
>= 85%
```

with higher targets for critical kernels.

---

# 69. PROPERTY-BASED TESTING

Generate large deterministic input spaces.

Use explicit seeds.

Check invariants instead of individual expected values when appropriate.

Store every failing seed.

---

# 70. METAMORPHIC TESTING

Use relationships such as:

### Linear systems

If:

[
Ax=b
]

then for scalar (c):

[
A(cx)=cb
]

where appropriate.

### Geometry

Translation should not change distances.

Rotation should preserve Euclidean norms.

### CFD

Uniform physical states should remain uniform.

### FEA

Consistent coordinate transformations should preserve physical response.

---

# 71. ANALYTICAL VERIFICATION

Where analytical solutions exist, compare numerically.

Report:

[
L\_1
]

[
L\_2
]

[
L\_\infty
]

errors.

Do not simply label test "accurate."

---

# 72. GRID CONVERGENCE

For discretized solvers test:

```
N
2N
4N
8N
```

Calculate empirical order:

[
p=
\frac{\log(e\_N/e\_{2N})}
{\log 2}
]

where mathematically meaningful.

---

# 73. CONSERVATION

For conservative algorithms measure:

# [ \epsilon\_Q

\frac{|Q(t)-Q(0)|}
{\max(|Q(0)|,\epsilon)}
]

Report actual error.

---

# 74. CONDITIONING

Measure condition sensitivity for applicable problems.

Do not mistake convergence on well-conditioned examples for general robustness.

Generate:

```
well-conditioned
moderately conditioned
ill-conditioned
near-singular
singular
```

cases.

---

# 75. EXTREME-SCALE NUMERICAL TESTING

Exercise representable scales such as:

```
1e-12
1e-9
1e-6
1e-3
1
1e3
1e6
1e9
1e12
```

only where mathematically meaningful.

Check scale sensitivity.

---

# 76. CROSS-IMPLEMENTATION VALIDATION

Where an independent trusted library is locally available, compare results.

Examples may include:

- NumPy;
- SciPy;
- standard analytical formulas;
- another independent implementation.

Use the external implementation as a comparison, not as a hidden replacement for SM32 code.

---

# 77. GOLDEN REFERENCE DATASETS

Create deterministic reference cases.

For each store:

```
input
expected output
tolerance
algorithm
version
hash
seed
```

---

# 78. REPRODUCIBILITY

Capture:

```
Python version
OS
architecture
CPU
GPU if used
NumPy version
SciPy version if used
compiler
environment
seed
thread count
backend
```

Repeated execution with identical configuration should remain reproducible within documented tolerances.

---

# 79. BENCHMARK SUITE

Create standardized benchmark families for:

- dense linear algebra;
- sparse operations;
- root solving;
- optimization;
- ODE;
- CFD;
- FEA;
- geometry;
- mesh;
- digital twin;
- UQ;
- graph;
- ML primitives;
- HPC.

---

# 80. PERFORMANCE BASELINE

SM32 must retain an auditable performance baseline.

For every benchmark record:

```
problem size
runtime
memory
iterations
error
backend
threads
```

---

# 81. REGRESSION GATES

A commit/build fails qualification if:

```
previous passing test now fails
accuracy materially degrades
conservation materially degrades
unexpected warning appears
memory leaks appear
performance catastrophically regresses
manifest integrity fails
```

unless explicitly reviewed and justified.

---

# 82. WARNINGS-AS-ERRORS

Run Celestial/SM32-owned source tests with warnings promoted to errors.

Target:

```
0 unexpected owned warnings
```

Separate dependency warnings.

---

# 83. STATIC ANALYSIS

Audit for:

```
undefined variables
dead code
unreachable branches
duplicate code
suspicious conditions
mutable defaults
incorrect exception handling
unchecked array dimensions
unsafe divisions
implicit broadcasting assumptions
unsafe casts
integer overflow
silent float overflow
resource leaks
```

---

# 84. DEAD IMPLEMENTATION DETECTION

Find functions that exist but can never be reached from any supported API or execution flow.

Classify:

```
PUBLIC
INTERNAL_ACTIVE
LEGACY
DEAD
```

Do not delete historical lineage automatically.

But authoritative SM32 runtime should avoid accidental dead code.

---

# 85. DEPENDENCY DISCIPLINE

Do not solve implementation gaps by importing an external library and merely wrapping it.

External libraries are allowed for:

- backend support;
- cross-validation;
- performance primitives;

but SM32-owned advertised algorithms should contain substantive SM32 logic.

---

# 86. SECURITY / ROBUSTNESS

For file/config/input interfaces:

- validate paths;
- avoid arbitrary execution;
- validate serialization;
- reject malformed state;
- limit unreasonable allocations where appropriate.

---

# 87. SERIALIZATION

Persist numerical states reliably.

Include:

```
schema version
data type
shape
endianness if relevant
metadata
checksum
algorithm version
```

Test round trips.

---

# 88. API STABILITY

Preserve SM31 APIs where reasonable.

Where change is unavoidable:

- create compatibility aliases;
- issue explicit deprecation;
- document migration.

---

# 89. DOCUMENTATION MUST FOLLOW CODE

Documentation cannot claim capability before the implementation gate passes.

Allowed status labels:

```
IMPLEMENTED
TESTED
NUMERICALLY_VERIFIED
BENCHMARKED
ENDURANCE_TESTED
EXTERNALLY_REPRODUCED
```

Each requires corresponding evidence.

---

# 90. NO SELF-AWARDED PARITY

Never declare:

```
better than PETSc
better than ANSYS
better than Fluent
better than Abaqus
better than OpenFOAM
better than PyTorch
better than JAX
better than AlphaFold
```

from architectural comparison alone.

Benchmark exact comparable functionality.

---

# 91. IMPLEMENTATION CLOSURE GATE

Before a capability can be marked **COMPLETE**, require:

```
[x] mathematical definition
[x] executable implementation
[x] input validation
[x] failure behavior
[x] unit tests
[x] edge tests
[x] property tests where applicable
[x] numerical verification
[x] meaningful coverage
[x] reproducibility
[x] benchmark
[x] documentation
```

Critical capabilities additionally require:

```
[x] stress test
[x] endurance participation
[x] performance characterization
```

---

# 92. ENDURANCE ARCHITECTURE

Expand the existing Celestial endurance system.

Mix active computational domains.

Example:

```
20% CFD
15% FEA
10% geometry/CAD
10% sparse linear algebra
10% optimization
10% advanced numerics
5% ODE
5% UQ
5% digital twin
5% graph/search
5% reproducibility/HPC
```

Adapt to actual implemented domains.

---

# 93. ENDURANCE TELEMETRY

Track:

```
elapsed time
iterations
operations per second
RSS
virtual memory
CPU
threads
file descriptors
temporary files
exceptions
warnings
NaN
Inf
invariant violations
solver failures
```

---

# 94. ENDURANCE TIERS

Perform only tiers the environment genuinely allows.

```
10 seconds
30 seconds
2 minutes
10 minutes
1 hour
8 hours
24 hours
72 hours
```

Every tier:

```
PASS
FAIL
INTERRUPTED
NOT_EXECUTED
```

Never extrapolate a 30-second pass into a 72-hour pass.

---

# 95. MEMORY LEAK ANALYSIS

Fit memory trend:

[
RSS(t)=a+bt
]

Report:

```
starting RSS
ending RSS
minimum RSS
maximum RSS
slope
```

Investigate monotonic growth.

---

# 96. SOAK DIVERSITY

Do not repeat one trivial solver endlessly.

Cycle:

```
different sizes
different seeds
different boundary conditions
different algorithms
different numerical scales
different backends
```

---

# 97. CHAOS / FAILURE TESTING

Where appropriate inject:

- malformed state;
- singular matrix;
- invalid mesh;
- impossible geometry;
- convergence failure;
- backend failure;
- interrupted execution;
- corrupted checkpoint.

Verify controlled recovery or deterministic failure.

---

# 98. CHECKPOINT / RESUME

For long computations where architecture supports it, implement state checkpointing.

Verify:

```
run
checkpoint
stop
reload
continue
```

matches uninterrupted execution within tolerance.

---

# 99. PARALLEL DETERMINISM

Where possible differentiate:

```
BITWISE_DETERMINISTIC
NUMERICALLY_DETERMINISTIC
NONDETERMINISTIC_BOUNDED
```

Document parallel reduction effects.

---

# 100. VALIDATION ENGINE

Create central:

`validation.py`

or deepen its existing equivalent.

Every validation run should produce structured evidence.

Example:

```
{
  "algorithm": "",
  "case": "",
  "expected": {},
  "actual": {},
  "error": {},
  "tolerance": {},
  "passed": true
}
```

---

# 101. EVIDENCE-FIRST ARCHITECTURE

Every claim must reference an artifact.

Example:

```
CLAIM:
Second-order CFD executes correctly.

EVIDENCE:
TEST-CFD-221
VALID-CFD-SOD-04
COVERAGE-CFD-32
ENDURANCE-SM32-02
```

---

# 102. QUALIFICATION DATABASE

Create:

`SM32_QUALIFICATION_LEDGER.json`

Track each capability:

```
implemented
tested
verified
benchmarked
stress-tested
endurance-tested
externally reproduced
```

No ambiguity.

---

# 103. SOURCE MANIFEST

Generate SHA-256 for every authoritative SM32 file.

Record:

```
path
bytes
sha256
role
version
```

---

# 104. PROVENANCE

Preserve:

```
SM31 CELESTIAL QP1
        ↓
SM32 inventory
        ↓
implementation closure
        ↓
solver hardening
        ↓
verification
        ↓
qualification
        ↓
SM32 authoritative payload
```

---

# 105. HISTORICAL SOURCE POLICY

If the merged artifact preserves historical source generations, retain them for lineage.

However explicitly identify:

```
AUTHORITATIVE_RUNTIME = SM32
```

Coverage and test qualification should separately report:

```
authoritative active runtime
historical preserved lineage
whole merged artifact
```

This prevents historical code from distorting active-runtime metrics.

---

# 106. IMPLEMENTATION DELTA

Produce exact statistics:

```
capabilities inventoried
concept-only before
reference implementations before
partial implementations before
fully implemented before

converted to functional
converted to verified
converted to benchmarked
remaining conceptual
remaining partial
```

---

# 107. SOURCE DELTA

Report:

```
files added
files modified
classes added
functions added
production LOC added
test LOC added
deleted LOC
```

Do not treat more lines as automatically better.

---

# 108. COMPLEXITY MANAGEMENT

Measure code complexity.

Flag:

- giant functions;
- deeply nested branches;
- excessive coupling;
- duplicated numerical logic;
- circular imports.

Refactor when it improves correctness and maintainability without destroying lineage.

---

# 109. MODULAR SOLVER CONTRACT

Major solvers should expose a common conceptual result structure:

```
solution
status
iterations
residual
converged
termination_reason
runtime
diagnostics
```

Use backward-compatible adapters where existing APIs differ.

---

# 110. NUMERICAL RESULT OBJECTS

Avoid returning only opaque arrays from advanced workflows.

Where appropriate include diagnostics.

Example:

```
SolverResult(
    solution=...,
    converged=True,
    iterations=37,
    residual_norm=...,
    termination_reason="tolerance",
)
```

---

# 111. TOLERANCE POLICY

Centralize numerical tolerances.

Distinguish:

```
absolute tolerance
relative tolerance
geometry tolerance
solver tolerance
physical floor
machine epsilon
```

No universal arbitrary `1e-6` everywhere.

---

# 112. PHYSICAL UNIT DISCIPLINE

Where engineering quantities exist, document units.

Do not silently mix:

```
m/mm
Pa/MPa
kg/g
s/ms
rad/degree
```

Test unit-sensitive interfaces.

---

# 113. DIMENSIONAL CONSISTENCY

Where feasible validate formulas dimensionally.

Especially:

- CFD;
- mechanics;
- heat transfer;
- dynamics;
- geometry.

---

# 114. BENCHMARKING AGAINST SM31

For every modified critical kernel compare:

```
SM31 result
SM32 result
accuracy delta
runtime delta
memory delta
```

SM32 must not unknowingly regress.

---

# 115. PERFORMANCE DOES NOT OVERRIDE CORRECTNESS

Optimization order:

```
correct
stable
verified
profiled
optimized
reverified
```

Never:

```
fast
then maybe correct
```

---

# 116. LARGE PROBLEM TESTING

Increase problem dimensions progressively until environment limitations are reached.

Record:

```
largest completed mesh
largest dense system
largest sparse system
largest geometry model
largest optimization dimension
peak memory
runtime
```

Do not call this universal scalability.

---

# 117. RESOURCE-AWARE EXECUTION

Detect available:

```
CPU cores
RAM
GPU
MPI
optional dependencies
```

Select safe execution scale.

Do not crash the host to prove scale.

---

# 118. EXTERNAL REPRODUCTION PACKAGE

Generate enough material for another system to reproduce qualification:

```
source
environment
dependency versions
commands
seeds
datasets
expected hashes
expected result ranges
```

---

# 119. CLEAN-ENVIRONMENT TEST

When possible, create a clean environment and reinstall only declared dependencies.

Then run:

```
imports
unit tests
integration tests
qualification subset
```

This detects hidden environment dependencies.

---

# 120. BUILD FROM ZERO

A new user must be able to reconstruct SM32 from the merged artifact without relying on undocumented local state.

---

# 121. SM32 FINAL ACCEPTANCE GATES

The successor cannot receive FULL qualification unless:

```
0 known P0 defects
0 known P1 correctness defects

all original SM31/QP1 tests PASS

all SM32 regression tests PASS

active-runtime statement coverage >= 98%
active-runtime branch coverage >= 95%

critical numerical kernels >= 99% meaningful statement coverage

0 unexpected owned warnings

0 NaN/Inf invariant escapes

0 silent solver failures

all advertised production APIs execute

all active algorithm registry entries resolve to real implementations

manifest verification = PASS

reproducibility = PASS

stress testing = PASS
```

Literal long-duration tiers remain separate.

---

# 122. IMPLEMENTATION COMPLETENESS SCORE

Calculate:

[
I=
\frac{
\sum\_i w\_i d\_i
}{
10\sum\_i w\_i
}
\times100
]

where:

- (d\_i) = implementation depth from 0–10;
- (w\_i) = importance weight.

Report separately:

```
Architecture Score
Implementation Score
Verification Score
Performance Score
Qualification Score
```

This prevents conceptual strength from hiding implementation shallowness.

---

# 123. NO 100/100 BY DECLARATION

A perfect score cannot be assigned because the prompt requested it.

Score must come from evidence.

If SM32 reaches:

```
Architecture        9.7
Implementation      9.4
Verification        9.5
Performance         8.8
Qualification       8.7
```

report those numbers rather than inventing 10/10.

---

The intended advancement is approximately:

```
SM31:
very broad architecture

growing qualification evidence

             ↓

SM32:

+

+

+

```

# 125. PRIORITY SYSTEM

## P0 — IMPLEMENTATION CLOSURE

1. inventory every capability;
2. identify conceptual/scaffold/reference-only implementations;
3. close unimplemented production paths;
4. repair correctness defects;
5. eliminate fake/empty implementations.

## P1 — NUMERICAL DEPTH

6. deepen numerical core;
7. deepen CFD;
8. deepen FEA;
9. deepen CAD/geometry;
10. deepen optimization;
11. deepen sparse solving;
12. deepen ODE/PDE.

## P1 — VERIFICATION

13. analytical validation;
14. convergence studies;
15. conservation tests;
16. property tests;
17. mutation tests;
18. edge testing.

## P2 — HPC / PERFORMANCE

19. vectorization;
20. sparse scaling;
21. multiprocessing;
22. MPI where available;
23. GPU where available;
24. profiling;
25. memory optimization.

## P2 — QUALIFICATION

26. active-runtime >=98/95 coverage;
27. warnings-as-errors;
28. stress;
29. endurance;
30. repeatability;
31. clean environment.

## P3 — RELEASE

32. capability ledger;
33. evidence package;
34. new manifest;
35. final merged artifact.

# 126. REQUIRED SM32 TEST SCALE

Do not target an arbitrary number merely for appearance.

However, SM32 should materially exceed the QP1 test corpus.

Prefer:

```
hundreds of meaningful deterministic tests
+
thousands of property-generated cases
+
numerical convergence matrices
+
benchmark matrices
+
stress iterations
```

Quality matters more than raw test count.

---

# 127. MINIMUM TEST TYPES PER CRITICAL SOLVER

Each critical solver should have:

```
normal case
boundary case
degenerate case
invalid input
known analytic solution
randomized valid case
conservation/invariant case
large problem
repeated execution
performance case
```

---

# 128. IMPLEMENTATION REVIEW LOOP

For every module:

```
READ
↓
CLASSIFY
↓
MODEL MATHEMATICS
↓
IMPLEMENT
↓
TEST
↓
BREAK
↓
FIX
↓
VERIFY
↓
BENCHMARK
↓
DOCUMENT
↓
QUALIFY
```

Repeat until acceptance gates are met.

---

# 129. RED-TEAM THE NUMERICAL CODE

Attempt deliberately difficult cases.

Look for:

- off-by-one indexing;
- incorrect broadcasting;
- orientation errors;
- zero division;
- catastrophic cancellation;
- overflow;
- underflow;
- unstable timestep;
- singularity;
- nonphysical states;
- convergence false positives;
- stale cache/state;
- race conditions.

The SM31 second-order CFD defect demonstrates why this is mandatory.

---

# 130. FALSE-CONVERGENCE DETECTION

A solver must not report convergence merely because:

```
max iterations reached
step became zero
NaN comparison returned false
gradient evaluation failed
```

Convergence must be explicitly demonstrated.

---

# 131. RESIDUAL VERIFICATION

On solver completion recompute the final residual independently where practical.

Do not rely exclusively on internal cached residual state.

---

# 132. DUAL-IMPLEMENTATION CHECKS

For especially important formulas, independently implement a slow reference path for testing.

Compare optimized path against reference.

Do not use the slow implementation in normal production execution.

---

# 133. ALGORITHM REGISTRY INTEGRITY

For every registered algorithm:

```
registration
→ import
→ constructor
→ execution
→ meaningful output
→ test
```

No orphan registry entries.

---

# 134. FEATURE CLAIM INTEGRITY

Search documentation for phrases such as:

```
supports
implements
provides
solves
accelerates
optimizes
distributed
GPU
parallel
advanced
production
```

Verify each statement against actual code.

Create:

`SM32_CLAIM_AUDIT.json`

---

# 135. CLAIM DOWNGRADING

When capability evidence is insufficient, downgrade documentation rather than exaggerating.

Example:

```
BEFORE:
"GPU accelerated solver"

AFTER:
"GPU backend interface; runtime validation not executed"
```

until hardware proof exists.

---

# 136. EXTERNAL LIBRARY COMPETITOR VALIDATION

Where technically possible compare isolated equivalent problems with established open implementations.

Do not attempt to prove universal superiority.

Compare exact tasks such as:

```
linear solve
ODE integration
Sod tube
element stiffness
optimization benchmark
```

---

# 137. SCIENTIFIC BENCHMARK SET

Use known benchmark functions where appropriate.

Optimization examples:

- Rosenbrock;
- Sphere;
- Himmelblau;
- Rastrigin where applicable.

Numerics:

- Hilbert-like conditioning stress;
- known polynomial roots;
- stiff/simple ODE references.

Geometry:

- exact line/circle primitives.

FEA:

- analytical bars/beams.

CFD:

- canonical conservation problems.

# 138. ERROR BUDGET

[
E\_{\text{total}}
\approx

\+

\+

\+
E\_{\text{model}}
]

where meaningful.

This forces separation of numerical errors.

# 145. SM32 SUCCESS DEFINITION

SM32 succeeds only if a reviewer can say:

> The impressive architecture inherited from SM31 is no longer carrying disproportionate conceptual weight. Core capabilities are now represented by substantial executable algorithms, clear mathematical formulations, robust numerical handling, extensive tests, analytical verification, benchmarks, reproducibility evidence, and qualification records.

# 146. WHAT NOT TO DO

Do NOT create SM32 by:

```
adding 5,000 algorithm names
adding thousands of empty classes
adding giant capability registries
adding theoretical descriptions without code
adding README claims
duplicating old implementation
wrapping NumPy/SciPy and calling it a new solver
generating fake benchmark data
inventing endurance results
renaming SM31 as SM32
```

That is specifically considered failure.

---

# 147. WHAT TO DO

SM32 should instead add:

```
deeper solver implementations
more mathematical completeness
more numerical safety
more convergence logic
more boundary conditions
more actual data structures
more valid backends
more physical invariants
more meaningful tests
more analytical validation
more profiling
more benchmarking
more reproducibility
more stress testing
more evidence
```

---

# 148. IMPLEMENTATION-FIRST EQUATION

Treat overall maturity approximately as:

# [ M

A^\alpha
I^\beta
V^\gamma
R^\delta
P^\epsilon
]

where:

- (A) = architecture;
- (I) = implementation;
- (V) = validation;
- (R) = reliability;
- (P) = performance evidence.

For SM32 assign:

[
\beta>\alpha
]

because architecture is already strong.

Implementation depth is now the dominant improvement variable.

Build the successor accordingly.

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass
import math,numpy as np
from .core import *
@dataclass
class Dual:
    val: float; der: float=0.0
    def __add__(self,o):o=o if isinstance(o,Dual) else Dual(float(o));return Dual(self.val+o.val,self.der+o.der)
    __radd__=__add__
    def __sub__(self,o):o=o if isinstance(o,Dual) else Dual(float(o));return Dual(self.val-o.val,self.der-o.der)
    def __rsub__(self,o):return Dual(float(o)).__sub__(self)
    def __mul__(self,o):o=o if isinstance(o,Dual) else Dual(float(o));return Dual(self.val*o.val,self.der*o.val+self.val*o.der)
    __rmul__=__mul__
    def __truediv__(self,o):o=o if isinstance(o,Dual) else Dual(float(o));return Dual(self.val/o.val,(self.der*o.val-self.val*o.der)/(o.val*o.val))
    def __rtruediv__(self,o):return Dual(float(o)).__truediv__(self)
    def __pow__(self,p):p=float(p);return Dual(self.val**p,p*self.val**(p-1)*self.der)
    def __neg__(self):return Dual(-self.val,-self.der)
def sin(x):return Dual(math.sin(x.val),math.cos(x.val)*x.der) if isinstance(x,Dual) else math.sin(x)
def cos(x):return Dual(math.cos(x.val),-math.sin(x.val)*x.der) if isinstance(x,Dual) else math.cos(x)
def exp(x):return Dual(math.exp(x.val),math.exp(x.val)*x.der) if isinstance(x,Dual) else math.exp(x)
def log(x):return Dual(math.log(x.val),x.der/x.val) if isinstance(x,Dual) else math.log(x)
def derivative(f,x):return float(f(Dual(float(x),1.)).der)
def jvp(f,x,v,eps=None):
    x=finite_array(x,'x',1);v=finite_array(v,'v',1)
    if x.shape!=v.shape:raise DimensionMismatch('x/v mismatch')
    eps=np.sqrt(np.finfo(float).eps)*(1+np.linalg.norm(x))/max(np.linalg.norm(v),np.finfo(float).tiny) if eps is None else float(eps);return (np.asarray(f(x+eps*v),float)-np.asarray(f(x-eps*v),float))/(2*eps)
def vjp(f,x,w,eps=None):
    x=finite_array(x,'x',1);w=finite_array(w,'w',1);eps=np.sqrt(np.finfo(float).eps) if eps is None else float(eps);g=np.empty_like(x)
    for i in range(len(x)):
        h=eps*max(1.,abs(x[i]));xp=x.copy();xm=x.copy();xp[i]+=h;xm[i]-=h;g[i]=w@(np.asarray(f(xp),float)-np.asarray(f(xm),float))/(2*h)
    return g

from __future__ import annotations
import time,tracemalloc

def benchmark(fn,*args,repeats=3,**kwargs):
    times=[];peaks=[];out=None
    for _ in range(repeats):
        tracemalloc.start();t=time.perf_counter();out=fn(*args,**kwargs);times.append(time.perf_counter()-t);_,peak=tracemalloc.get_traced_memory();peaks.append(peak);tracemalloc.stop()
    return {'min_time':min(times),'mean_time':sum(times)/len(times),'max_time':max(times),'peak_memory':max(peaks),'repeats':repeats,'result':out}

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass,field
import numpy as np
from .core import *
try:
    from supermoon31.cad import BSplineCurve,NURBSCurve,Frame,Point3,Vector3
except Exception:
    BSplineCurve=NURBSCurve=Frame=Point3=Vector3=None
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Vertex: id:int; point:tuple[float,float,float]
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Edge: id:int; v0:int; v1:int
@dataclass
class Wire: id:int; edges:list[int]
@dataclass
class Face: id:int; outer_wire:int; inner_wires:list[int]=field(default_factory=list)
@dataclass
class Shell: id:int; faces:list[int]=field(default_factory=list)
@dataclass
class Solid: id:int; shells:list[int]=field(default_factory=list)
@dataclass
class BRepModel:
    vertices:dict[int,Vertex]=field(default_factory=dict);edges:dict[int,Edge]=field(default_factory=dict);wires:dict[int,Wire]=field(default_factory=dict);faces:dict[int,Face]=field(default_factory=dict);shells:dict[int,Shell]=field(default_factory=dict);solids:dict[int,Solid]=field(default_factory=dict)
    def validate(self):
        errors=[]
        for e in self.edges.values():
            if e.v0 not in self.vertices or e.v1 not in self.vertices:errors.append(f'edge {e.id} missing vertex')
            elif e.v0==e.v1:errors.append(f'edge {e.id} degenerate')
        for w in self.wires.values():
            if not w.edges:errors.append(f'wire {w.id} empty')
            if any(e not in self.edges for e in w.edges):errors.append(f'wire {w.id} missing edge')
        for f in self.faces.values():
            if f.outer_wire not in self.wires or any(w not in self.wires for w in f.inner_wires):errors.append(f'face {f.id} invalid wire')
        for s in self.shells.values():
            if any(f not in self.faces for f in s.faces):errors.append(f'shell {s.id} missing face')
        for s in self.solids.values():
            if any(sh not in self.shells for sh in s.shells):errors.append(f'solid {s.id} missing shell')
        return {'valid':not errors,'errors':errors,'counts':{k:len(getattr(self,k)) for k in ['vertices','edges','wires','faces','shells','solids']}}
    def add_vertex(self,p):
        p=tuple(map(float,p));
        if len(p)!=3 or not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):raise InvalidInput('finite 3D point required')
        i=max(self.vertices.keys(),default=-1)+1;self.vertices[i]=Vertex(i,p);return i
    def add_edge(self,v0,v1):
        i=max(self.edges.keys(),default=-1)+1;self.edges[i]=Edge(i,int(v0),int(v1));return i

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass
import numpy as np, math
from .core import *
try:
    from supermoon31.cfd import euler_1d, sod_initial, primitive, cons, rusanov, hll
except Exception:
    euler_1d=sod_initial=primitive=cons=rusanov=hll=None
@dataclass
class Euler2DResult:
    U: np.ndarray; steps: int; time: float; min_density: float; min_pressure: float; max_cfl: float; conservation: dict

def primitive2d(U,gamma=1.4):
    U=finite_array(U,'U')
    if U.shape[-1]!=4:raise DimensionMismatch('Euler2D state requires [...,4]')
    rho=U[...,0];mx=U[...,1];my=U[...,2];E=U[...,3]
    if np.any(rho<=0):raise NonPhysicalState('density <= 0')
    u=mx/rho;v=my/rho;p=(gamma-1)*(E-.5*rho*(u*u+v*v))
    if np.any(p<=0) or not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):raise NonPhysicalState('pressure <= 0/nonfinite')
    return rho,u,v,p

def cons2d(rho,u,v,p,gamma=1.4):
    rho=np.asarray(rho,float);u=np.asarray(u,float);v=np.asarray(v,float);p=np.asarray(p,float);E=p/(gamma-1)+.5*rho*(u*u+v*v);return np.stack([rho,rho*u,rho*v,E],axis=-1)

def flux_x(U,gamma=1.4):
    rho,u,v,p=primitive2d(U,gamma);E=U[...,3];return np.stack([rho*u,rho*u*u+p,rho*u*v,u*(E+p)],axis=-1)
def flux_y(U,gamma=1.4):
    rho,u,v,p=primitive2d(U,gamma);E=U[...,3];return np.stack([rho*v,rho*u*v,rho*v*v+p,v*(E+p)],axis=-1)
def rusanov_face(UL,UR,axis,gamma=1.4):
    rL,uL,vL,pL=primitive2d(UL,gamma);rR,uR,vR,pR=primitive2d(UR,gamma);cL=np.sqrt(gamma*pL/rL);cR=np.sqrt(gamma*pR/rR);FL=flux_x(UL,gamma) if axis==0 else flux_y(UL,gamma);FR=flux_x(UR,gamma) if axis==0 else flux_y(UR,gamma);velL=uL if axis==0 else vL;velR=uR if axis==0 else vR;s=np.maximum(np.abs(velL)+cL,np.abs(velR)+cR);return .5*(FL+FR)-.5*s[...,None]*(UR-UL)
def euler_2d_cartesian(U0,dx,dy,t_end,cfl=.4,gamma=1.4,max_steps=100000,periodic=True):
    U=finite_array(U0,'U0').copy();dx=float(dx);dy=float(dy);t_end=float(t_end)
    if U.ndim!=3 or U.shape[-1]!=4 or min(U.shape[:2])<2 or dx<=0 or dy<=0 or t_end<0:raise InvalidInput('invalid 2-D Euler grid')
    init=np.sum(U,axis=(0,1))*dx*dy;t=0.;steps=0;maxcfl=0.;mr=math.inf;mp=math.inf
    while t<t_end-1e-15:
        rho,u,v,p=primitive2d(U,gamma);c=np.sqrt(gamma*p/rho);speedx=float(np.max(np.abs(u)+c));speedy=float(np.max(np.abs(v)+c));dt=cfl/(speedx/dx+speedy/dy);dt=min(dt,t_end-t)
        if not math.isfinite(dt) or dt<=0:raise CFLViolation('invalid timestep')
        if periodic:
            Fx=rusanov_face(U,np.roll(U,-1,axis=1),0,gamma);Fy=rusanov_face(U,np.roll(U,-1,axis=0),1,gamma);U=U-dt/dx*(Fx-np.roll(Fx,1,axis=1))-dt/dy*(Fy-np.roll(Fy,1,axis=0))
        else:
            Ug=np.pad(U,((1,1),(1,1),(0,0)),mode='edge');Fx=rusanov_face(Ug[:, :-1],Ug[:,1:],0,gamma);Fy=rusanov_face(Ug[:-1],Ug[1:],1,gamma);U=U-dt/dx*(Fx[1:-1,1:]-Fx[1:-1,:-1])-dt/dy*(Fy[1:,1:-1]-Fy[:-1,1:-1])
        rho,u,v,p=primitive2d(U,gamma);mr=min(mr,float(rho.min()));mp=min(mp,float(p.min()));maxcfl=max(maxcfl,float(dt*(speedx/dx+speedy/dy)));t+=dt;steps+=1
        if steps>max_steps:raise ConvergenceFailure('max_steps exceeded')
    final=np.sum(U,axis=(0,1))*dx*dy;cons_err={k:float(abs(final[i]-init[i])) for i,k in enumerate(['mass','momentum_x','momentum_y','energy'])}
    return Euler2DResult(U,steps,t,mr,mp,maxcfl,cons_err)

def navier_stokes_viscous_flux_2d(grad_u,grad_v,grad_T,mu,kappa):
    gu=finite_array(grad_u,'grad_u',1);gv=finite_array(grad_v,'grad_v',1);gT=finite_array(grad_T,'grad_T',1)
    if len(gu)!=2 or len(gv)!=2 or len(gT)!=2:raise DimensionMismatch('2-D gradients required')
    div=gu[0]+gv[1];tau_xx=mu*(2*gu[0]-2*div/3);tau_yy=mu*(2*gv[1]-2*div/3);tau_xy=mu*(gu[1]+gv[0]);q=-kappa*gT
    return {'tau':np.array([[tau_xx,tau_xy],[tau_xy,tau_yy]]),'heat_flux':q}

<<<SM32_FILE path="src/supermoon32/core.py" sha256="fbc344fd02cf1017048e5018d78fb21f3450c27fdce12081787615b1bca4665b" bytes="3818">>>
from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from enum import Enum
from typing import Any, Iterable
import math, time
import numpy as np

class SM32Error(Exception):
    """Base error for SUPER MOON 32."""
class InvalidInput(SM32Error):
    """InvalidInput error."""
class DimensionMismatch(SM32Error):
    """DimensionMismatch error."""
class NonPhysicalState(SM32Error):
    """NonPhysicalState error."""
class DegenerateGeometry(SM32Error):
    """DegenerateGeometry error."""
class SingularSystem(SM32Error):
    """SingularSystem error."""
class IllConditionedSystem(SM32Error):
    """IllConditionedSystem error."""
class ConvergenceFailure(SM32Error):
    """ConvergenceFailure error."""
class CFLViolation(SM32Error):
    """CFLViolation error."""
class InvariantViolation(SM32Error):
    """InvariantViolation error."""
class BackendUnavailable(SM32Error):
    """BackendUnavailable error."""
class NumericalOverflow(SM32Error):
    """NumericalOverflow error."""

class Determinism(str, Enum):
    BITWISE='BITWISE_DETERMINISTIC'; NUMERICAL='NUMERICALLY_DETERMINISTIC'; BOUNDED='NONDETERMINISTIC_BOUNDED'

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TolerancePolicy:
    atol: float=1e-12; rtol: float=1e-9; geometry: float=1e-10; solver: float=1e-10; physical_floor: float=1e-12
    def close(self,a,b,scale=1.0): return abs(float(a)-float(b)) <= self.atol*max(1.0,float(scale)) + self.rtol*max(abs(float(a)),abs(float(b)))
DEFAULT_TOLERANCE=TolerancePolicy()

@dataclass
class SolverResult:
    solution: Any
    converged: bool
    iterations: int=0
    residual_norm: float=math.inf
    termination_reason: str=''
    runtime: float=0.0
    diagnostics: dict[str,Any]=field(default_factory=dict)
    history: list[float]=field(default_factory=list)
    status: str='OK'

@dataclass
class ValidationRecord:
    algorithm: str; case: str; expected: Any; actual: Any; error: Any; tolerance: Any; passed: bool; metadata: dict[str,Any]=field(default_factory=dict)


def finite_array(x,name='array',ndim=None):
    a=np.asarray(x,float)
    if ndim is not None and a.ndim!=ndim: raise DimensionMismatch(f'{name} must have ndim={ndim}, got {a.ndim}')
    if a.size==0: raise InvalidInput(f'{name} must be non-empty')
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): raise InvalidInput(f'{name} contains NaN/Inf')
    return a

def finite_scalar(x,name='value'):
    try:v=float(x)
    except Exception as e: raise InvalidInput(f'{name} must be numeric') from e
    if not math.isfinite(v): raise InvalidInput(f'{name} must be finite')
    return v

def kahan_sum(values: Iterable[float])->float:
    s=0.0;c=0.0
    for x in values:
        y=float(x)-c;t=s+y;c=(t-s)-y;s=t
    return s

def stable_norm(x)->float:
    a=finite_array(x,'x').ravel(); m=float(np.max(np.abs(a)))
    if m==0:return 0.0
    return m*math.sqrt(float(np.dot(a/m,a/m)))

def safe_normalize(x,tol=DEFAULT_TOLERANCE):
    a=finite_array(x,'x'); n=stable_norm(a)
    if n <= tol.geometry*max(1.0,float(np.max(np.abs(a)))): raise DegenerateGeometry('cannot normalize near-zero vector')
    return a/n

def stable_polyval(coefficients,x):
    c=finite_array(coefficients,'coefficients',1); xv=finite_scalar(x,'x'); y=0.0
    for a in c:y=y*xv+float(a)
    if not math.isfinite(y): raise NumericalOverflow('polynomial evaluation overflow')
    return y

def scale_aware_tolerance(*values,base=None):
    base=np.finfo(float).eps if base is None else float(base); scale=max([1.0]+[abs(float(v)) for v in values]); return base*scale

def check_invariants(**checks):
    failed=[k for k,v in checks.items() if not bool(v)]
    if failed: raise InvariantViolation('failed invariants: '+', '.join(failed))
    return True

def timed_call(fn,*args,**kwargs):
    t=time.perf_counter(); out=fn(*args,**kwargs); return out,time.perf_counter()-t

from dataclasses import dataclass,field
import time, numpy as np
from .core import *
@dataclass
class StateTwin:
    state: np.ndarray; covariance: np.ndarray; parameters: dict=field(default_factory=dict); history:list=field(default_factory=list); provenance:dict=field(default_factory=dict)
    def __post_init__(self):
        self.state=finite_array(self.state,'state',1);self.covariance=finite_array(self.covariance,'covariance',2)
        if self.covariance.shape!=(len(self.state),len(self.state)):raise DimensionMismatch('covariance dimension mismatch')
        if np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh((self.covariance+self.covariance.T)/2))<-1e-12:raise InvalidInput('covariance must be PSD')
    def predict(self,F,Q=None,control=None,B=None,timestamp=None):

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass
import itertools,math
from .core import InvalidInput
@dataclass
class DiscoveryResult:
    candidate:object;score:float;evaluated:int;history:list
def exhaustive_discovery(search_space,evaluator,minimize=True,max_evals=None):
    best=None;bs=math.inf if minimize else -math.inf;hist=[];n=0
    for c in search_space:
        s=float(evaluator(c));n+=1;hist.append((c,s))
        if (minimize and s<bs) or ((not minimize) and s>bs):best,bs=c,s
        if max_evals is not None and n>=max_evals:break
    if n==0:raise InvalidInput('empty search space')
    return DiscoveryResult(best,bs,n,hist)
def expression_program_search(constants,ops,target,max_depth=2):
    vals=[(str(c),float(c)) for c in constants];allv=list(vals)
    best=(None,math.inf,None);evaluated=0
    for _ in range(max_depth):
        nxt=[]
        for (ea,a),(eb,b) in itertools.product(allv,allv):
            for name,op in ops.items():
                try:v=float(op(a,b))
                except Exception:continue
                if not math.isfinite(v):continue
                e=f'({ea}{name}{eb})';err=abs(v-target);evaluated+=1
                if err<best[1]:best=(e,err,v)
                nxt.append((e,v))
        allv=(allv+nxt)[-500:]
        if best[1]==0:break
    return {'expression':best[0],'error':best[1],'value':best[2],'evaluated':evaluated}

from __future__ import annotations

try:import psutil
except Exception:psutil=None

def telemetry():
    if psutil:
        p=psutil.Process();m=p.memory_info();return {'time':time.time(),'rss':m.rss,'vms':m.vms,'threads':p.num_threads(),'fds':p.num_fds() if hasattr(p,'num_fds') else None}
    return {'time':time.time(),'rss':None,'vms':None,'threads':None,'fds':None}
def mixed_iteration(seed=0):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed);A=rng.normal(size=(8,8));A=A.T@A+np.eye(8);b=rng.normal(size=8);r=cg(as_csr(A),b,tol=1e-9,max_iter=100)
    o=bfgs(lambda x:(x[0]-1)**2+2*(x[1]+2)**2,[0,0],tol=1e-7,max_iter=30)
    od=rk4(lambda t,y:-y,(0,.05),[1.],.01)
    rho=np.ones((4,4));u=np.zeros_like(rho);v=np.zeros_like(rho);p=np.ones_like(rho);U=cons2d(rho,u,v,p);cf=euler_2d_cartesian(U,.25,.25,.002)
    ba=bar1d([0,1],[(0,1)],2e11,1e-4,{1:1000},[0]);ori=orientation2d([0,0],[1,0],[0,1])
    bad=not(r.converged and o.converged and np.all(np.isfinite(od.y)) and cf.min_density>0 and ba.residual_norm<1e-8 and ori==1)
    return {'bad':bad,'cg_residual':r.residual_norm,'opt_residual':o.residual_norm,'cfd_steps':cf.steps}
def run_endurance(duration_seconds,sample_interval=.5,report_path=None):
    duration_seconds=float(duration_seconds)
    if duration_seconds<=0:raise ValueError('positive duration required')
    start=time.monotonic();last=start;rows=[telemetry()];iters=errors=invariants=0
    while time.monotonic()-start<duration_seconds:
        try:
            rr=mixed_iteration(iters)
            if rr['bad']:invariants+=1
        except Exception:errors+=1
        iters+=1;now=time.monotonic()
        if now-last>=sample_interval:rows.append(telemetry());last=now
    elapsed=time.monotonic()-start;rss=[x['rss'] for x in rows if x['rss'] is not None];rep={'requested_seconds':duration_seconds,'elapsed_seconds':elapsed,'iterations':iters,'iterations_per_second':iters/elapsed,'errors':errors,'invariant_failures':invariants,'telemetry':rows,'rss_min':min(rss) if rss else None,'rss_max':max(rss) if rss else None,'rss_drift':rss[-1]-rss[0] if len(rss)>1 else 0,'passed':errors==0 and invariants==0 and elapsed>=duration_seconds}
    if report_path:Path(report_path).write_text(json.dumps(rep,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
    return rep

@dataclass
class BarResult:
    displacement: np.ndarray; reactions: np.ndarray; stress: np.ndarray; strain: np.ndarray; residual_norm: float

def bar1d(nodes,elements,E,A,loads=None,fixed=None):
    x=finite_array(nodes,'nodes',1);elements=np.asarray(elements,int);n=len(x);E=float(E);A=float(A)
    if E<=0 or A<=0 or elements.ndim!=2 or elements.shape[1]!=2:raise InvalidInput('invalid bar model')
    K=np.zeros((n,n));stress_ops=[]
    for e,(i,j) in enumerate(elements):
        if i<0 or j<0 or i>=n or j>=n or i==j:raise InvalidInput('invalid element')
        L=abs(x[j]-x[i])
        if L<=DEFAULT_TOLERANCE.geometry:raise DegenerateGeometry('zero-length bar')
        ke=E*A/L*np.array([[1,-1],[-1,1]],float);dof=[i,j];K[np.ix_(dof,dof)]+=ke;stress_ops.append((i,j,L))
    f=np.zeros(n)
    if loads:
        for i,v in loads.items():f[int(i)]+=float(v)
    fixed=sorted(set(int(i) for i in (fixed or [])));free=np.array([i for i in range(n) if i not in fixed],int)
    if len(free)==0:u=np.zeros(n)
    else:
        Kff=K[np.ix_(free,free)]

def triangle3_plane_stress_stiffness(coords,E,nu,thickness=1.0):

def assemble(elements,n_dof):
    K=np.zeros((n_dof,n_dof))
    for dofs,ke in elements:
        d=np.asarray(dofs,int);ke=np.asarray(ke,float)
        if ke.shape!=(len(d),len(d)):raise DimensionMismatch('element stiffness mismatch')
        K[np.ix_(d,d)]+=ke
    return K
def solve_linear(K,f,fixed=(),prescribed=None):
    K=finite_array(K,'K',2);f=finite_array(f,'f',1);n=len(f)

def orientation2d(a,b,c,tol=DEFAULT_TOLERANCE):
    a,b,c=map(lambda z:finite_array(z,'point',1),[a,b,c])
    if not(len(a)==len(b)==len(c)==2):raise DimensionMismatch('2D points required')
    cross=(b[0]-a[0])*(c[1]-a[1])-(b[1]-a[1])*(c[0]-a[0]);scale=max(1.,distance(a,b)*distance(a,c));eps=tol.geometry*scale
    return 1 if cross>eps else -1 if cross<-eps else 0
def segment_intersection(a,b,c,d,tol=DEFAULT_TOLERANCE):
    a,b,c,d=[np.asarray(x,float) for x in (a,b,c,d)];r=b-a;s=d-c;den=r[0]*s[1]-r[1]*s[0];q=c-a;numt=q[0]*s[1]-q[1]*s[0];numu=q[0]*r[1]-q[1]*r[0];eps=tol.geometry*max(1.,np.linalg.norm(r)*np.linalg.norm(s))
    if abs(den)<=eps:return None
    t=numt/den;u=numu/den
    if -tol.geometry<=t<=1+tol.geometry and -tol.geometry<=u<=1+tol.geometry:return a+t*r
    return None
def project_point_segment(p,a,b):
    p,a,b=[np.asarray(x,float) for x in (p,a,b)];d=b-a;den=float(d@d)
    if den<=DEFAULT_TOLERANCE.geometry**2:raise DegenerateGeometry('zero-length segment')
    t=float((p-a)@d/den);tc=min(1.,max(0.,t));q=a+tc*d;return q,tc,distance(p,q)

    p=np.asarray(p,float);o=np.asarray(origin,float);n=safe_normalize(normal);return p-n*float((p-o)@n)
def aabb(points):
    P=finite_array(points,'points',2);return P.min(axis=0),P.max(axis=0)
def rotation_matrix_2d(theta):
    c=math.cos(theta);s=math.sin(theta);return np.array([[c,-s],[s,c]])
def triangle_area(a,b,c):
    a,b,c=[np.asarray(x,float) for x in (a,b,c)];
    if len(a)==2:return abs((b[0]-a[0])*(c[1]-a[1])-(b[1]-a[1])*(c[0]-a[0]))/2
    return np.linalg.norm(np.cross(b-a,c-a))/2

from __future__ import annotations
import heapq, collections, math
from .core import *
def _adj(graph,u):return graph.get(u,{}) if isinstance(graph,dict) else {}
def bfs(graph,start):
    q=collections.deque([start]);seen={start};order=[]
    while q:
        u=q.popleft();order.append(u)
        neigh=_adj(graph,u);it=neigh.keys() if isinstance(neigh,dict) else neigh
        for v in it:
            if v not in seen:seen.add(v);q.append(v)
    return order
def dfs(graph,start):
    st=[start];seen=set();order=[]
    while st:
        u=st.pop()
        if u in seen:continue
        seen.add(u);order.append(u);neigh=_adj(graph,u);it=list(neigh.keys() if isinstance(neigh,dict) else neigh);st.extend(reversed(it))
    return order
def dijkstra(graph,start,goal=None):
    dist={start:0.};prev={};pq=[(0.,start)]
    while pq:
        d,u=heapq.heappop(pq)
        if d!=dist[u]:continue
        if u==goal:break
        neigh=_adj(graph,u)
        items=neigh.items() if isinstance(neigh,dict) else ((v,1.) for v in neigh)
        for v,w in items:
            w=float(w)
            if w<0:raise InvalidInput('negative weights not supported')
            nd=d+w
            if nd<dist.get(v,math.inf):dist[v]=nd;prev[v]=u;heapq.heappush(pq,(nd,v))
    return dist,prev
def path_from_prev(prev,start,goal):
    if goal!=start and goal not in prev:return None
    p=[goal]
    while p[-1]!=start:p.append(prev[p[-1]])
    return list(reversed(p))
def connected_components(graph):
    nodes=set(graph)
    for u in graph:
        neigh=_adj(graph,u);nodes.update(neigh.keys() if isinstance(neigh,dict) else neigh)
    comps=[]
    while nodes:
        s=next(iter(nodes));c=set(bfs(graph,s));comps.append(c);nodes-=c
    return comps

def threaded_map(fn,items,max_workers=None):
    with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers) as ex:return list(ex.map(fn,items))
def multiprocess_map(fn,items,max_workers=None):
    with concurrent.futures.ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers) as ex:return list(ex.map(fn,items))
def benchmark_scaling(fn,arg,workers=(1,2,4),repeats=1,backend='thread'):
    rows=[]
    for p in workers:
        t=time.perf_counter()
        if p==1:[fn(arg) for _ in range(repeats)]
        elif backend=='thread':threaded_map(fn,[arg]*repeats,p)
        else:multiprocess_map(fn,[arg]*repeats,p)
        dt=time.perf_counter()-t;rows.append({'workers':p,'time':dt})
    t1=rows[0]['time']
    for r in rows:r['speedup']=t1/r['time'] if r['time'] else float('inf');r['efficiency']=r['speedup']/r['workers']
    return rows

                for j in e:

    def boundary_edges(self):

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass
import numpy as np, math
from .core import *
def softmax(x,axis=-1):
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    if x.size==0 or np.any(np.isnan(x)) or np.any(np.isposinf(x)): raise InvalidInput('softmax input contains invalid values')
    m=np.max(x,axis=axis,keepdims=True)
    if np.any(~np.isfinite(m)): raise InvalidInput('softmax row has no finite entries')
    e=np.exp(x-m);return e/np.sum(e,axis=axis,keepdims=True)
def attention(Q,K,V,mask=None):
    Q=finite_array(Q,'Q');K=finite_array(K,'K');V=finite_array(V,'V')
    if Q.ndim!=2 or K.ndim!=2 or V.ndim!=2 or Q.shape[1]!=K.shape[1] or K.shape[0]!=V.shape[0]:raise DimensionMismatch('attention shape mismatch')
    scores=Q@K.T/math.sqrt(Q.shape[1])
    if mask is not None:
        m=np.asarray(mask,bool)
        if m.shape!=scores.shape:raise DimensionMismatch('mask shape mismatch')
        scores=np.where(m,scores,-np.inf)
        if np.any(np.all(~m,axis=1)):raise InvalidInput('fully masked query row')
    W=softmax(scores,axis=1);return W@V,W
@dataclass
class Dense:
    W: np.ndarray; b: np.ndarray
    @classmethod
    def init(cls,in_dim,out_dim,seed=0):
        rng=np.random.default_rng(seed);W=rng.normal(scale=math.sqrt(2/(in_dim+out_dim)),size=(in_dim,out_dim));return cls(W,np.zeros(out_dim))
    def forward(self,x):
        x=finite_array(x,'x');return x@self.W+self.b

def relu(x):return np.maximum(np.asarray(x,float),0.)
def mse(yhat,y):
    yhat=np.asarray(yhat,float);y=np.asarray(y,float);return float(np.mean((yhat-y)**2))
def numerical_gradient(f,x,eps=1e-6):
    x=np.asarray(x,float).copy();g=np.empty_like(x)
    it=np.nditer(x,flags=['multi_index'])
    while not it.finished:
        i=it.multi_index;old=x[i];x[i]=old+eps;fp=f(x);x[i]=old-eps;fm=f(x);x[i]=old;g[i]=(fp-fm)/(2*eps);it.iternext()
    return g
def linear_regression_train(X,y,lr=.05,epochs=1000,tol=1e-10):
    X=finite_array(X,'X',2);y=finite_array(y,'y',1)
    if len(X)!=len(y):raise DimensionMismatch('X/y mismatch')
    w=np.zeros(X.shape[1]);b=0.;hist=[]
    for k in range(int(epochs)):
        pred=X@w+b;err=pred-y;loss=float(np.mean(err*err));hist.append(loss);gw=2*X.T@err/len(y);gb=2*np.mean(err);w-=lr*gw;b-=lr*gb
        if np.linalg.norm(np.r_[gw,gb])<=tol:break
    return {'weights':w,'bias':float(b),'loss':hist[-1],'history':hist,'epochs':len(hist)}
def cosine_beta_schedule(T,s=0.008):
    T=int(T)
    if T<=0:raise InvalidInput('T positive')
    t=np.linspace(0,T,T+1)/T;abar=np.cos((t+s)/(1+s)*np.pi/2)**2;abar/=abar[0];beta=1-abar[1:]/abar[:-1];return np.clip(beta,1e-8,.999)
def q_learning_step(Q,s,a,r,s2,alpha=.1,gamma=.99):
    Q=np.asarray(Q,float);target=float(r)+gamma*np.max(Q[s2]);td=target-Q[s,a];Q[s,a]+=alpha*td;return float(td)

from __future__ import annotations
import numpy as np,time
from .core import *

def fixed_point_coupling(update_x,update_y,x0,y0,tol=1e-9,max_iter=200,relaxation=.5):
    x=np.asarray(x0,float).copy();y=np.asarray(y0,float).copy();hist=[];t=time.perf_counter();w=float(relaxation)
    if not 0<w<=1:raise InvalidInput('relaxation must be in (0,1]')
    for k in range(max_iter+1):
        xn=np.asarray(update_x(x,y),float);yn=np.asarray(update_y(xn,y),float);rx=float(np.linalg.norm(xn-x));ry=float(np.linalg.norm(yn-y));r=max(rx,ry);hist.append(r)
        if r<=tol:return SolverResult((x,y),True,k,r,'coupling_tolerance',time.perf_counter()-t,{'x_residual':rx,'y_residual':ry},hist)
        if k==max_iter:break
        x=(1-w)*x+w*xn;y=(1-w)*y+w*yn
    return SolverResult((x,y),False,max_iter,hist[-1],'max_iter',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist,status='NOT_CONVERGED')
def conservative_transfer(source_values,source_weights,target_weights):
    s=finite_array(source_values,'source_values',1);sw=finite_array(source_weights,'source_weights',1);tw=finite_array(target_weights,'target_weights',1)
    if len(s)!=len(sw):raise DimensionMismatch('source values/weights mismatch')
    total=float(s@sw)
    if np.any(tw<0) or np.sum(tw)<=0:raise InvalidInput('positive target weights required')
    # Constant target density that preserves integrated quantity exactly.
    out=np.full(len(tw),total/np.sum(tw));return out,{'source_total':total,'target_total':float(out@tw),'error':float(abs(out@tw-total))}

from __future__ import annotations
import math,time
import numpy as np
from .core import *

def lu_factor(A,pivot_tol=1e-15):
    A=finite_array(A,'A',2).copy(); n,m=A.shape
    if n!=m: raise DimensionMismatch('A must be square')
    P=np.arange(n)
    for k in range(n-1):
        p=k+int(np.argmax(np.abs(A[k:,k])))
        if abs(A[p,k]) <= pivot_tol*max(1.0,float(np.linalg.norm(A,np.inf))): raise SingularSystem('singular pivot')
        if p!=k: A[[k,p]]=A[[p,k]];P[[k,p]]=P[[p,k]]
        A[k+1:,k]/=A[k,k]; A[k+1:,k+1:]-=np.outer(A[k+1:,k],A[k,k+1:])
    if abs(A[-1,-1]) <= pivot_tol*max(1.0,float(np.linalg.norm(A,np.inf))): raise SingularSystem('singular pivot')
    return P,np.tril(A,-1)+np.eye(n),np.triu(A)

def forward_substitution(L,b):
    L=finite_array(L,'L',2); b=finite_array(b,'b')
    n=L.shape[0]; y=np.zeros_like(b,dtype=float)
    for i in range(n): y[i]=(b[i]-L[i,:i]@y[:i])/L[i,i]
    return y

def backward_substitution(U,b):
    U=finite_array(U,'U',2); b=finite_array(b,'b'); n=U.shape[0];x=np.zeros_like(b,dtype=float)
    for i in range(n-1,-1,-1):
        if abs(U[i,i])<np.finfo(float).tiny: raise SingularSystem('zero diagonal')
        x[i]=(b[i]-U[i,i+1:]@x[i+1:])/U[i,i]
    return x

def lu_solve(A,b):
    P,L,U=lu_factor(A); bb=finite_array(b,'b'); y=forward_substitution(L,bb[P]); return backward_substitution(U,y)

def qr_householder(A):
    A=finite_array(A,'A',2).copy(); m,n=A.shape; Q=np.eye(m);R=A
    for k in range(min(m,n)):
        x=R[k:,k]; norm=np.linalg.norm(x)
        if norm==0: continue
        alpha=-math.copysign(norm,x[0] if x[0]!=0 else 1.0); v=x.copy();v[0]-=alpha;vn=np.linalg.norm(v)
        if vn==0:continue
        v/=vn; R[k:]-=2*np.outer(v,v@R[k:]); Q[:,k:]-=2*np.outer(Q[:,k:]@v,v)
    R[np.abs(R)<1e-15]=0.0; return Q,R

def cholesky(A,tol=1e-14):
    A=finite_array(A,'A',2); n,m=A.shape
    if n!=m or not np.allclose(A,A.T,rtol=1e-10,atol=1e-12): raise InvalidInput('A must be symmetric square')
    L=np.zeros_like(A)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i+1):
            s=A[i,j]-np.dot(L[i,:j],L[j,:j])
            if i==j:
                if s<=tol: raise InvalidInput('A is not positive definite')
                L[i,j]=math.sqrt(s)
            else:L[i,j]=s/L[j,j]
    return L

def svd(A): return np.linalg.svd(finite_array(A,'A',2),full_matrices=False)
def eig(A): return np.linalg.eig(finite_array(A,'A',2))
def least_squares(A,b,rcond=None): return np.linalg.lstsq(finite_array(A,'A',2),finite_array(b,'b'),rcond=rcond)
def pseudoinverse(A,rcond=1e-12):
    U,s,Vt=svd(A); cutoff=rcond*(s[0] if len(s) else 1.0); si=np.where(s>cutoff,1/s,0); return (Vt.T*si)@U.T

def condition_estimate(A):
    A=finite_array(A,'A',2); return float(np.linalg.cond(A))

def iterative_refinement(A,b,x0=None,tol=1e-12,max_iter=10):
    A=finite_array(A,'A',2);b=finite_array(b,'b');x=lu_solve(A,b) if x0 is None else finite_array(x0,'x0').copy(); hist=[];t=time.perf_counter()
    for k in range(max_iter+1):
        r=b-A@x; rn=float(np.linalg.norm(r));hist.append(rn)
        if rn<=tol*(np.linalg.norm(b)+1): return SolverResult(x,True,k,rn,'tolerance',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist)
        if k==max_iter:break
        x+=lu_solve(A,r)
    return SolverResult(x,False,max_iter,hist[-1],'max_iter',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist,status='NOT_CONVERGED')

def bisection(f,a,b,tol=1e-12,max_iter=200):
    a=finite_scalar(a);b=finite_scalar(b);fa=float(f(a));fb=float(f(b));t=time.perf_counter();hist=[]
    if not all(map(math.isfinite,[fa,fb])): raise InvalidInput('non-finite function value')
    if fa==0:return SolverResult(a,True,0,0,'exact_endpoint')
    if fb==0:return SolverResult(b,True,0,0,'exact_endpoint')
    if fa*fb>0: raise InvalidInput('root not bracketed')
    for k in range(1,max_iter+1):
        c=(a+b)/2;fc=float(f(c)); hist.append(abs(fc))
        if not math.isfinite(fc): raise InvalidInput('function produced NaN/Inf')
        if abs(fc)<=tol or abs(b-a)<=tol*(1+abs(c)): return SolverResult(c,True,k,abs(fc),'tolerance',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist)
        if fa*fc<=0:b,fb=c,fc
        else:a,fa=c,fc
    return SolverResult(c,False,max_iter,abs(fc),'max_iter',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist,status='NOT_CONVERGED')

def secant(f,x0,x1,tol=1e-12,max_iter=100):
    x0=float(x0);x1=float(x1);f0=float(f(x0));f1=float(f(x1));hist=[];t=time.perf_counter()
    for k in range(1,max_iter+1):
        den=f1-f0
        if abs(den)<=np.finfo(float).eps*max(1,abs(f0),abs(f1)): raise ConvergenceFailure('secant denominator collapsed')
        x2=x1-f1*(x1-x0)/den;f2=float(f(x2));hist.append(abs(f2))
        if not math.isfinite(f2): raise InvalidInput('function produced NaN/Inf')
        if abs(f2)<=tol:return SolverResult(x2,True,k,abs(f2),'tolerance',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist)
        x0,f0,x1,f1=x1,f1,x2,f2
    return SolverResult(x1,False,max_iter,abs(f1),'max_iter',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist,status='NOT_CONVERGED')

def newton_scalar(f,df,x0,tol=1e-12,max_iter=100,damping=True):
    x=float(x0);hist=[];t=time.perf_counter()
    for k in range(max_iter+1):
        fx=float(f(x));hist.append(abs(fx))
        if not math.isfinite(fx):raise InvalidInput('function produced NaN/Inf')
        if abs(fx)<=tol:return SolverResult(x,True,k,abs(fx),'tolerance',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist)
        if k==max_iter:break
        d=float(df(x))
        if not math.isfinite(d) or abs(d)<=np.finfo(float).eps:raise ConvergenceFailure('zero/nonfinite derivative')
        step=-fx/d; alpha=1.0
        if damping:
            while alpha>1e-8:
                fn=float(f(x+alpha*step))
                if math.isfinite(fn) and abs(fn)<abs(fx):break
                alpha*=0.5
        x+=alpha*step
    return SolverResult(x,False,max_iter,hist[-1],'max_iter',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist,status='NOT_CONVERGED')

def brent(f,a,b,tol=1e-12,max_iter=100):
    # Safeguarded secant/bisection hybrid.
    a=float(a);b=float(b);fa=float(f(a));fb=float(f(b));
    if fa*fb>0:raise InvalidInput('root not bracketed')
    hist=[];t=time.perf_counter()
    for k in range(1,max_iter+1):
        if abs(fb-fa)>np.finfo(float).eps:maxsec=b-fb*(b-a)/(fb-fa)
        else:maxsec=(a+b)/2
        lo,hi=min(a,b),max(a,b)
        c=maxsec if lo < maxsec < hi else (a+b)/2
        fc=float(f(c));hist.append(abs(fc))
        if abs(fc)<=tol or abs(b-a)<=tol*(1+abs(c)):return SolverResult(c,True,k,abs(fc),'tolerance',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist)
        if fa*fc<=0:b,fb=c,fc
        else:a,fa=c,fc
    return SolverResult(c,False,max_iter,abs(fc),'max_iter',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist,status='NOT_CONVERGED')

def finite_difference_jacobian(F,x,eps=None):
    x=finite_array(x,'x',1);f0=finite_array(F(x),'F(x)',1);J=np.empty((len(f0),len(x))); eps=math.sqrt(np.finfo(float).eps) if eps is None else float(eps)
    for j in range(len(x)):
        h=eps*max(1.0,abs(x[j]));xp=x.copy();xm=x.copy();xp[j]+=h;xm[j]-=h;J[:,j]=(finite_array(F(xp),'F+',1)-finite_array(F(xm),'F-',1))/(2*h)
    return J

def newton_system(F,x0,jac=None,tol=1e-10,max_iter=50):
    x=finite_array(x0,'x0',1).copy();hist=[];t=time.perf_counter()
    for k in range(max_iter+1):
        f=finite_array(F(x),'F',1);rn=float(np.linalg.norm(f));hist.append(rn)
        if rn<=tol:return SolverResult(x,True,k,rn,'tolerance',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist)
        if k==max_iter:break
        J=finite_difference_jacobian(F,x) if jac is None else finite_array(jac(x),'J',2)
        try:step=np.linalg.solve(J,-f)
        except np.linalg.LinAlgError as e:raise SingularSystem('singular Jacobian') from e
        alpha=1.0
        while alpha>1e-8 and np.linalg.norm(F(x+alpha*step))>=rn:alpha*=0.5
        x=x+alpha*step
    return SolverResult(x,False,max_iter,hist[-1],'max_iter',time.perf_counter()-t,history=hist,status='NOT_CONVERGED')

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass,field
import numpy as np, math
from .core import *
@dataclass
class ODEResult:
    t: np.ndarray; y: np.ndarray; accepted: int; rejected: int=0; events: list=field(default_factory=list)

def _prep(fun,t_span,y0,dt):
    t0,tf=map(float,t_span); y=finite_array(y0,'y0',1).copy();dt=float(dt)
    if tf<t0 or dt<=0:raise InvalidInput('require tf>=t0 and dt>0')
    return t0,tf,y,dt

def euler(fun,t_span,y0,dt):
    t,tf,y,dt=_prep(fun,t_span,y0,dt);ts=[t];ys=[y.copy()]
    while t<tf-1e-15:
        h=min(dt,tf-t);y=y+h*finite_array(fun(t,y),'f',1);t+=h;ts.append(t);ys.append(y.copy())
    return ODEResult(np.array(ts),np.array(ys),len(ts)-1)
def midpoint(fun,t_span,y0,dt):
    t,tf,y,dt=_prep(fun,t_span,y0,dt);ts=[t];ys=[y.copy()]
    while t<tf-1e-15:
        h=min(dt,tf-t);k1=finite_array(fun(t,y),'k1',1);k2=finite_array(fun(t+h/2,y+h*k1/2),'k2',1);y=y+h*k2;t+=h;ts.append(t);ys.append(y.copy())
    return ODEResult(np.array(ts),np.array(ys),len(ts)-1)
def rk4(fun,t_span,y0,dt):
    t,tf,y,dt=_prep(fun,t_span,y0,dt);ts=[t];ys=[y.copy()]
    while t<tf-1e-15:
        h=min(dt,tf-t);k1=np.asarray(fun(t,y),float);k2=np.asarray(fun(t+h/2,y+h*k1/2),float);k3=np.asarray(fun(t+h/2,y+h*k2/2),float);k4=np.asarray(fun(t+h,y+h*k3),float);y=y+h*(k1+2*k2+2*k3+k4)/6;t+=h
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):raise NumericalOverflow('ODE produced NaN/Inf')
        ts.append(t);ys.append(y.copy())
    return ODEResult(np.array(ts),np.array(ys),len(ts)-1)
def rk45(fun,t_span,y0,dt=1e-2,rtol=1e-7,atol=1e-10,event=None,max_steps=100000):
    t,tf,y,h=_prep(fun,t_span,y0,dt);ts=[t];ys=[y.copy()];accepted=rejected=0;events=[];prev_event=float(event(t,y)) if event else None
    # Dormand-Prince 5(4)
    for _ in range(max_steps):
        if t>=tf-1e-15:break
        h=min(h,tf-t);k1=np.asarray(fun(t,y),float);k2=np.asarray(fun(t+h/5,y+h*k1/5),float);k3=np.asarray(fun(t+3*h/10,y+h*(3*k1/40+9*k2/40)),float);k4=np.asarray(fun(t+4*h/5,y+h*(44*k1/45-56*k2/15+32*k3/9)),float);k5=np.asarray(fun(t+8*h/9,y+h*(19372*k1/6561-25360*k2/2187+64448*k3/6561-212*k4/729)),float);k6=np.asarray(fun(t+h,y+h*(9017*k1/3168-355*k2/33+46732*k3/5247+49*k4/176-5103*k5/18656)),float);k7=np.asarray(fun(t+h,y+h*(35*k1/384+500*k3/1113+125*k4/192-2187*k5/6784+11*k6/84)),float)
        y5=y+h*(35*k1/384+500*k3/1113+125*k4/192-2187*k5/6784+11*k6/84);y4=y+h*(5179*k1/57600+7571*k3/16695+393*k4/640-92097*k5/339200+187*k6/2100+k7/40)
        scale=atol+rtol*np.maximum(np.abs(y),np.abs(y5));err=float(np.sqrt(np.mean(((y5-y4)/scale)**2)))
        if err<=1:
            told=t;t+=h;y=y5;accepted+=1;ts.append(t);ys.append(y.copy())
            if event:
                ev=float(event(t,y))
                if prev_event==0 or ev==0 or prev_event*ev<0:events.append({'t_left':told,'t_right':t,'value':ev})
                prev_event=ev
        else:rejected+=1
        fac=5.0 if err==0 else min(5.0,max(.2,.9*err**(-.2)));h*=fac
        if h<=np.finfo(float).tiny:raise ConvergenceFailure('adaptive step underflow')
    else:raise ConvergenceFailure('RK45 max steps exceeded')
    return ODEResult(np.array(ts),np.array(ys),accepted,rejected,events)
